## Demo 12 — A domain nobody built this for, reconciled ★

**The claim**: a compliance domain the platform has never heard of — its
vocabulary, its law, its matching rules, its reconciliation and its review
queue — arrives as **files**, and no Rust or TypeScript changes.

**What you can show**, end to end and reproducible today
(`scripts/verify-pack-load.sh`, then `graph-owl-reconcile packs/gst`):

1. `graph-owl-load-pack packs/gst` — the pack's IRI becomes namespace 1024, its
   nine predicates become registry terms, and 19 subjects land in named import
   graphs. Load `packs/hospitality` beside it and the two get **distinct codes,
   no shared vocabulary, and the same loader**.
2. `graph-owl-reconcile packs/gst` — **all six rules** from the GST demo's own
   findings table, 9 findings opened, each citing the provision it rests on and
   carrying the triples behind it.
3. **Run it again**: `opened: 0, alreadyOpen: 3`. Dismiss one with a reason,
   run a third time, and it stays dismissed.
4. Console → Review → **Findings** — one queue, `pack` as a column, accept or
   dismiss-with-reason, the rule and evidence in the detail pane.
5. **The one to actually show a CA**: INV-2001 and INV-2002 are both July 2020
   invoices, one with a 5% value delta and one with 20%. Only the second is a
   finding, and it cites Notification 75/2019-CT — because the cap in force
   then was 10%, read from `law/rule-36-4.ttl` by traversal. The same 5% delta
   on a 2026 invoice *is* a finding, citing 40/2021-CT. Same rule, same query,
   different answers, because the law is data and the graph records when.
6. `graph-owl-erpnext "Rescue Mission"` — a schema **invented at run time** in a
   live Frappe/ERPNext instance becomes a queryable vocabulary, `Link` fields
   resolving as real edges.
7. `scripts/demo.sh --gst` does 1–4 in one command, additively, against the
   already-running bank estate.

### Epic 105 — domain neutrality
- [~] **105** **DN-1 through P5 shipped, 10 August 2026.** The blocker was never the pack format: it was that a namespace code could only be minted by editing `graph-owl-core`. `NamespaceResolver`/`RuntimeNamespaces` (codes 1024–65534), a `namespace_registry` table, `POST /namespaces`, `POST /predicates` and `POST /graph/import/rdf` close it; `scripts/check-namespace-neutrality.py` fails the build if a domain name reappears as a constant. **Two hardcodings were found by running real data through, not by reading**: the pack loaded but every SPARQL query returned zero rows, because `is_vocabulary_namespace` was a literal list of three medical namespaces — the same Epic 104 hardcoding in a second location, now generalized to `>= RUNTIME_START`. And `FILTER NOT EXISTS` reported **all four** invoices missing including two that match perfectly, because pushdown does not descend into it; the query is rewritten as `OPTIONAL` + `!BOUND` and the engine-side fix is named, not hidden. **P5's findings runtime shipped with a correction of its own**: the first index keyed `(pack, label, subject)` and was partial on `status = 'pending'`, so a dismissal came straight back on the next run over identical data — found by running the real reconciliation twice around a decision, fixed by putting a SHA-256 evidence digest in the identity (`V60`), which draws the line where it belongs: same conclusion from same facts is already decided, changed facts are a new situation. **The GST pack is complete against its own spec**: all six findings
(`PotentialMismatch`, `AmountMismatch`, `ITCNotAvailable`, `Reversed`,
`GstinTransposition`, `PaymentOverdue`), the dated Rule 36(4) spine resolved by
traversal, purchases and payments as events with time anchors, 26 assertions in
`scripts/verify-gst-reconciliation.sh` **each checked in both directions**, and
a fifteen-question evaluation set with answer keys written before any agent
exists. Two things SPARQL could not do were measured rather than assumed — the
engine has no date support in expressions at all (`date > date` evaluates to
unbound), and no notion of string similarity — so the query does the join and
the runtime does the arithmetic, with both thresholds declared in the manifest.
Writing the evaluation set found a real false-accusation bug: the 180-day rule
flagged *every* unpaid invoice, including one six days old. **P6 (fusion)
characterized rather than rebuilt, 11 August 2026**: the platform plan's
fusion primitives (candidate generation, blocking, scoring, decision) are
`105b`'s native reconcile engine under different names — `[findings.similarity]`
is the blocking/scoring band, the SPARQL join is candidate generation, the
finding itself is the fused decision. **P7's traversal half shipped 10 August
2026** (`105e`) — `Catalog::finding_evidence_graph`, a real Postgres
traversal bug found and fixed along the way (every reconstructed `Sid` was
hardcoded to the `dsc` namespace, silently hiding every domain-pack predicate
from traversal). **P7's interpretation half shipped 11 August 2026** (`105g`,
both slices): every evidence-graph node now reports which source document
asserted it (`Catalog::node_sources`), and `GstinTransposition` — the one GST
finding whose whole premise is a missing edge — surfaces the unlinked second
Supplier by resolving it by value (`Catalog::near_miss_node`, a new
`[findings.similarity].resolveBy` pack-config field) rather than pretending a
generic "missing-hop" mechanism was needed for all six findings, when five of
them compute their own gaps at finding-creation time. Two real bugs found
along the way, not anticipated in either plan: a `var` field silently dropped
on write (caught only by the real-Postgres test, not the fake-graph unit
tests), and a mutation-testing survivor traceable to `HashMap`-ordered fake
results making "which of two seeded flakes wins" nondeterministic. **P9's
aggregation half characterized, 11 August 2026**: `SUM`/`COUNT`/`AVG`/`GROUP
BY`/`DISTINCT` already work end to end through the adopted `spareval`
evaluator — five characterization tests against the platform plan's own
worked example (three invoices at ₹100/₹200/₹300 summing to ₹600) all pass
against the *existing* code, confirming the plan's own claim that this was
"probably not work at all" rather than an unbuilt gap. **P8's first real
slice and F4 both shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105h`): "Event → Legal Rule →
Obligation → Due Date → Risk" turns out to be `[findings.span]` read forward
instead of only backward — `passes_span` already computed
`(judged_on - start).num_days() > exceeds_days` as a boolean and silently
discarded the due date itself. `Catalog::obligation_calendar` computes it for
every open `PaymentOverdue`-shaped row, and the console's new **Obligations**
route renders it — checked before building, not assumed: the platform doc's
full P8 needs a temporal graph engine (§7) that does not exist, so this is
the due-date half of the one obligation GST already has, not the general
law/rules runtime. **P8's §7 temporal-engine prerequisite closed to its real
minimal form, 11 August 2026** (`105i`): `graph_owl_resolution::temporal::in_force_at`
generalizes `amount-mismatch.sparql`'s own string-comparison trick — which
provision was in force on an invoice date — into a Rust primitive proven
against GST's own `rule-36-4.ttl` fixture *and* against a domain with no
relationship to GST at all (a freight rate card), the same two-proof
discipline the hospitality proof-pack already established. Date-window
*traversal* (the graph walk itself filtered by validity, not just resolving
already-fetched rows) is named as a real, separate, still-open gap rather
than folded into "done." **P9's query planner (§8) characterized as already
built, 11 August 2026** (`105j`): the pushdown planner, the shared SPARQL/Cypher
algebra (Epic 7b/7c), and `Catalog`'s existing `sparql`/`cypher`/`asset_subgraph`
entry points already are the planner the doc describes; the only missing
piece is routing an abstract tool request to one of them, which is
definitionally P10's job (the tools don't exist yet to route). **P9's
GraphRAG retrieval (§10) gained a real assembly primitive**:
`Catalog::graph_context` generalizes the walk-plus-provenance shape
`finding_evidence_graph` already had beyond a finding's own subject to any
seed — proven against a non-GST asset fixture. Mutation testing on it found
something worth recording rather than a gap: cargo-mutants generated exactly
one candidate mutant (whole-function replacement) and it was unviable,
because `GraphContext`/`GraphContextNode` deliberately have no `Default` for
a mutant to fall back to — the same property that made every earlier
survivor this session possible (`TriplePattern`, `Flake`, `Asset` all derive
`Default`) is precisely what this new code does not have. **P10's first
intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105k`): `traverse()`, the first
of the platform doc's eight named MCP tools. Deliberately wraps the
pre-existing, already-authorized `Catalog::asset_subgraph` rather than
`graph_context` (`105j`) — `graph_context` takes no principal and has no
policy check at all, and pack-domain data has no authorization model yet, so
routing an agent-facing tool through it would have been a real, silent
security gap rather than a missing feature. Scoped to catalog assets only
for exactly that reason. Mutation testing on the dispatch/argument-parsing/
budget-fitting code (`--lib`) found nine real gaps on the first pass, all
inside the `Fits` impl, closed by two more dispatcher tests plus two direct
calls onto the trait methods — direct because `budget::fit`'s ladder always
fully drains edges before ever touching nodes, so `drop_entities`'s own
dangling-edge cleanup can never run against a non-empty edge list through a
real dispatcher call. Mutation testing on the real production adapter
(`CatalogContext::traverse`, proven against a real Postgres asset via a new
integration test, not the unit-level `Fixture` double) found one gap that
was *not* closed: there is no asset-to-asset relationship-creation path
anywhere in this codebase to seed a real multi-hop fixture with — the legacy
`POST /tables/{id}/relationships` route is a different entity/id space from
`Asset` (Epic 31's own documented gotcha), and `LINK_LINEAGE` is a declared
write tool that is not actually wired to write anything yet, proven by its
own existing test. Recorded as a real, structural gap rather than
worked around. **P10's second intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026**
(`105l`): `find_evidence()` wraps the pre-existing `finding_evidence_graph`
(`105e`) — unlike `traverse`, this needed no new authorization decision,
because the HTTP route it wraps is already, deliberately, not
visibility-checked per finding ("a finding is queue data a reviewer needs
to see to do the job... not a new privilege"), so wrapping it exposes
nothing beyond what the same credential can already reach over HTTP. Two
real-Postgres tests, reusing `evidence_graph.rs`'s own GST fixtures
(`PotentialMismatch` for the ordinary walk, `GstinTransposition` for the
near-miss path, since the first fixture alone never exercises
`near_miss_node`'s `Some` branch at all). Mutation testing closed 12 gaps
in the token-budget ladder and left two open, honestly: the `max_hops`
bound (no multi-hop pack-domain fixture exists anywhere in this codebase,
the identical gap `traverse`'s own report already named) and the near-miss
exclusion guard's "always true" mutant (both available fixtures happen to
have the near-miss candidate genuinely unreached, so the guard's real value
and the mutant agree by coincidence rather than by being checked — killing
it needs a fixture this slice did not construct). **P10's third
intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105m`): `explain()` wraps
`Catalog::explain_fact` — the same "already ships over HTTP, so no fresh
authorization decision" reasoning `105l` already stated for
`find_evidence`, not re-derived. `explanation_json`/`flake_json` are
hand-written free functions rendering the identical recursive shape
`GET /reasoning/explain`'s own handler already produces, duplicated rather
than shared, because neither `graph_owl_reasoning::Explanation` nor
`graph_owl_core::flake::Flake` can derive `Serialize` (the same convention
`Sid`/`EdgeRef` already follow) and `graph-owl-mcp` does not depend on
`graph-owl-server`. The budget ladder is unusual by design: no lever
shrinks an explanation at all, because a dropped chain or premise would
misrepresent the derivation rather than merely shrink it — an oversized
answer is returned accurate and over budget instead. Mutation testing on
the dispatch/rendering code found 3 gaps (the rendering functions were
never reached by the unit-level fixture, which hands back an already-built
JSON value rather than routing a real `Explanation` through them) and
closed them with direct tests covering all four `Explanation` variants.
Mutation testing on the real adapter, proven against `reasoning.rs`'s own
three-level ontology fixture, came back clean — zero open gaps, the
cleanest of the three tools shipped so far, because `explain` has no
hop-count or bounds parameter for a fixture-shaped gap to hide behind.
**P10's fourth intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105n`):
`reconcile()` wraps `Catalog::reconcile_pack` — the first P10 tool that
needed a genuinely new authorization check rather than inheriting one,
because unlike every prior tool on this trait it **writes**: it evaluates
a pack's rules and records what they conclude in the review queue.
`ContextSource::reconcile` checks `principal.is_admin` itself, matching
the HTTP route this wraps exactly (the identical gate `GET
/packs/{pack}/finding-rules` already applies, confirming this is an
established convention here rather than a one-off call); a non-admin gets
the same `Ok(None)` every denial on this trait already returns. It reuses
`graph_owl_api::ReconcileOutcome` directly as its wire type (already
`Serialize`, from `105b`) and skips `budget::Fits` entirely, matching
`Outcome::Wrote`'s own precedent — five scalar fields have nothing to
shrink. Mutation testing on the dispatch code came back clean (0 missed).
Mutation testing on the real adapter, proven against a new real-Postgres
test built from two hand-constructed `Principal`s (one admin, one not) run
against the same catalog, found the property no earlier test in this
codebase could exercise: every existing HTTP test resolves to
`Principal::system()` in open mode regardless of which subject a bearer
token claims, so this is the first proof that a genuinely non-admin
caller is refused. Zero open gaps.
**P10's fifth intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105o`):
`analytics()` — degree centrality, connected components and orphan
detection over the same bounded, already-authorized neighbourhood
`traverse()` walks, built by explicit user instruction rather than left
deferred. The apparent tension with Epic 38's own "never run analytics
live over the whole graph per request" is resolved, not overridden: `00e`'s
"purity boundary" text already distinguishes a bounded walk from an
unbounded whole-graph computation as "a real operational distinction," so
scoping to `asset_subgraph`'s own already-bounded, already-authorized
walk satisfies both the architecture and the instruction at once.
`PageRank` is deliberately excluded — its meaning is whole-graph-relative
by construction (Epic 38 decision 6's own "on probation" caution), and a
number computed over an arbitrary bounded neighbourhood would look like
PageRank without meaning what PageRank means. Two real bugs surfaced
before shipping, not after: a mutation run on the `Catalog`-layer unit
test found the per-node flake fetch's `s: Some(sid)` scoping was
unobservable with too few seeded flakes (fixed by seeding a flake
belonging to a node outside the walked set, which the fix must now
exclude); and the real-Postgres adapter test's first run failed because
the wire node id was assumed to be the asset's FQN when it is actually
the graph subject's raw id (a UUID) — matching, not diverging from,
`TraversalNode`'s own pre-existing convention, which the two tools now
share so an agent can correlate degree data back to a walk it already has.
Mutation testing on the dispatch/wire code came back clean (0 missed,
after closing the one gap `TraversalContext::shorten_detail` already
proved was the shape of: a permanently-`false` lever whose flip to `true`
is absorbed by `budget::fit`'s own no-progress check, closed by asserting
the same contract directly rather than through the dispatcher). Mutation
testing on the real adapter, proven against a new real-Postgres test,
found the identical structural gap `105k` already recorded and did not
close for `traverse` — no asset-to-asset relationship-creation path
exists in this codebase to build a multi-hop fixture with — inherited
rather than reintroduced.
**P10's sixth intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105p`):
`run_rule()` — the single-rule counterpart to `reconcile()`, evaluating
exactly one of a pack's registered rules by `(pack, label)` rather than
the whole pack, for an agent that already knows which rule it wants
re-checked. Reuses `finding_rules` rather than adding a per-label registry
lookup (`FindingRuleRegistry` has no such method, and filtering a pack's
already-small rule list costs nothing a new one would save), reuses
`ReconcileOutcome`/`Outcome::Reconciled` directly rather than a new wire
type (`evaluated` — one rule vs. the whole pack — is what tells the two
calls apart), and inherits `reconcile`'s admin gate rather than
re-deriving it, since evaluating one rule writes to the review queue the
same way evaluating all of them does. The Catalog-layer RED test found two
real, pre-existing properties of `Catalog::sparql`'s own scoping that no
earlier `graph-owl-api`-level test had exercised (because `reconcile_pack`
itself — the method `run_rule` is the single-rule counterpart to — has
never had one, only HTTP-level coverage): every scan is filtered to
`visible`, built from real asset rows in storage, so a flake asserted
under a hand-picked `Sid` with no matching asset row is invisible to every
query; and `Flake::assert` (what `upsert_asset`'s own projection uses)
sets `cx: None` (the default graph), so a hand-built flake asserted under
a *named* graph is invisible to a `GRAPH`-less `SELECT` even once
`visible` is fixed — the second failure was found only by comparing
`facts_scanned` (1, correct) against `rows` (empty) in a debug trace, the
same "measure directly rather than reason" discipline this project's own
build-loop notes already establish for timing puzzles, applied here to a
query-semantics one. Mutation testing came back clean at all three layers
(`lib.rs`: 4 mutants, 2 caught, 2 unviable, 0 missed; `Catalog::run_rule`:
2 mutants, 1 caught, 1 unviable, 0 missed; the real adapter, proven
against `reconcile.rs`'s own GST fixture: 3 mutants, 2 caught, 1 unviable,
0 missed) — every unviable mutant the same `Ok(Some(Default::default()))`
shape every P10 tool has hit, because `ReconcileOutcome` derives no
`Default` by `105b`'s own deliberate choice.
**P10's seventh intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105q`):
`resolve_entity()` — the platform doc's entity-linking primitive, and the
first P10 tool with no existing Catalog method to wrap. The design
question answered before any code: full-text search
(`search_assets_for`, already the `search_assets` tool) already exists,
so what does this add? Answer: a normalized similarity score, not a
relevance rank — "what mentions this text" and "how alike is this asset's
name to that text" are different questions, and only the second gives an
agent a number it can threshold on. Retrieves candidates through the
identical policy-filtered `search_assets_for` call `search` already uses
(no second search index), then re-scores each hit with
`graph_owl_resolution::rule_match::similarity` (trigrams, `n = 3` — the
same default Postgres's own `pg_trgm` extension uses on the storage
engine this project already runs on, not a number chosen for this tool).
No `Option` wrapper on the trait method, matching `search`'s own
signature exactly: an empty result is a real, complete answer. A new wire
type (`ResolvedEntityContext`/`ResolvedCandidate`) rather than reusing
`SearchHit`, which carries a trust summary entity resolution has no use
for. The RED test hand-derived the expected trigram similarity for two
real candidates before running anything (`orders` shares 3 of 10 possible
trigrams with the query `"ord"`, score `0.3`; `coordination` shares 1 of
18, score `1/18 ≈ 0.0556`) and a third seeded asset sharing no substring
with the query was asserted absent — all three passed on the first run,
confirming the hand-derived math rather than the code being what was
tested. Mutation testing came back clean at all three layers (`lib.rs`: 9
mutants, 7 caught, 2 unviable, 0 missed, after closing the same
permanently-`false`-lever gap `TraversalContext`/`AnalyticsContext`
already established for `shorten_detail`, this time for two levers at
once; `Catalog::resolve_entity`: 2 mutants, 1 caught, 1 unviable, 0
missed; the real adapter: 1 mutant, 1 caught, 0 missed — fewer candidates
than every other P10 tool because this one has no admin gate to mutate,
matching `search`'s own open posture).
**P10's eighth and last intelligence tool shipped, 11 August 2026**
(`105r`): `calculate_risk()` — completing Epic 105 P10. The scope
question answered before any code: the only existing "risk" concept
anywhere in this codebase is the obligation calendar's own
`DUE_SOON_HORIZON_DAYS = 30` display bucket, and that constant's own
comment already says what it is — "there is no pack-config field for it
yet, so it is a display threshold, not a business rule any finding
depends on." Building an agent tool on top of it would hand a UI
convenience to an agent as computed fact. Resolved by refusing to invent
a score: the only non-invented signal this system has is
`Obligation::days_remaining` (real calendar arithmetic, already computed
by `obligation_calendar`, P8/F4), so `calculate_risk()` is a filter over
that existing computation — narrowed to one subject, nothing
synthesized. Open, not admin-gated (unlike `reconcile`/`run_rule`): this
only reads, so it inherits `search`/`resolve_entity`'s posture rather
than a write-triggered gate. No `Option` wrapper, no new wire type
(`Obligation` reused directly, matching `Outcome::Recalled`'s own bare
`Vec<T>` shape with no `budget::Fits` impl), no not-found (pack-domain
subjects have no identity check to run, so "nothing open" and "no such
subject" are one real, empty answer). The Catalog-layer RED test reused
`run_rule_tests`'s own fixture shape exactly (real asset rows, `cx: None`
flakes) with two subjects seeded far enough in the past and future that
the test is deterministic regardless of when it runs, proving both the
overdue subject's negative `days_remaining` and that the *other*
subject's own answer excludes it. Mutation testing came back clean at all
three layers (`lib.rs`: 4 mutants, 2 caught, 2 unviable — both on
pre-existing generic dispatch machinery incidentally in the diff's line
range, not `calculate_risk`-specific; `Catalog::calculate_risk`: 3
mutants, 2 caught, 1 unviable; the real adapter, proven against
`obligation_calendar.rs`'s own GST fixture: 2 mutants, 1 caught, 1
unviable, 0 missed anywhere). All eight platform-doc intelligence tools —
`traverse`, `find_evidence`, `explain`, `reconcile`, `analytics`,
`run_rule`, `resolve_entity`, `calculate_risk` — are now shipped,
mutation-tested, and proven against a real adapter.
**P11's first agent shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105s`,
`integrations/langchain/examples/gst_investigation_agent.py`): a real
LangGraph `create_agent` tool-calling loop over `GraphOwlToolkit`'s full
P10 surface, answering evaluation question 14 ("which invoices would
become compliant if paid today?") — a question no existing reference
script covers, since `reconcile_agent.py`'s `QUESTION_LABELS` table only
routes 1-5 and the eval set's own "Running it" section says outright
"there is no agent yet" for 12-15. **Placement was the real question**:
`graph_owl_langchain`'s own `pyproject.toml` says "never a chain, an
agent, or a runtime," and `00j`/the roadmap's refusal table say the same
about a shipped agent runtime — asked the user rather than routing around
either, and the answer was to build it with LangChain/LangGraph in Python,
placed outside what ships (`integrations/langchain/examples/`, sibling to
`tests/`, excluded by `packages.find`'s `graph_owl_langchain*` scope and
untouched by `scripts/check-examples-purity.py`, which only scans the
top-level `examples/` tree). Floors bumped to the current major generation
(`langchain-core>=1.5`, `langgraph>=1.2`, plus a new `langchain>=1.3`)
because the API this agent uses, `langchain.agents.create_agent`, replaces
`create_react_agent` (deprecated as of LangGraph v1.0) — Epic 43's own
existing test was left on the old API rather than migrated, since it still
passes and that migration is unrelated scope. `investigate()` is a
three-line pass-through (`create_agent(model, tools,
system_prompt=SYSTEM_PROMPT).invoke(...)`), proven by two scripted-model
tests matching `test_langgraph_integration.py`'s own established pattern:
the full `resolve_entity` → `calculate_risk` → answer path with real P10
wire shapes, and that `SYSTEM_PROMPT` actually reaches the model's first
call (`create_agent` does not carry the system message in its returned
state, checked directly before writing the assertion). No mutmut report —
tried it, and it doesn't fit: mutmut resolves a mutant's module key from
the file's repo-relative path, but the test imports the script as a bare
top-level module via `sys.path.insert`, the same convention
`reconcile_agent.py`'s and `triage.py`'s own tests already use because
neither script belongs to an installable package; neither of those two
scripts has ever had mutmut run against it either.
**P12's scoring math shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105t`,
`examples/gst-reconcile/eval_scoring.py`): finding-level precision/recall
and a Wilson interval, matching `questions.md`'s own "Scoring" section
exactly rather than approximating it — `score_finding` is a set
comparison (duplicate invoice names get no extra credit, per the eval
set's own worked example) and `wilson_interval` uses the closed-form
Wilson formula (accurate at small `n`, unlike the normal approximation),
with `z = 1.96` documented as the standard 95% constant rather than a
number invented for this system. **Scoped to what has a real,
machine-checkable answer today**: ten of the fifteen questions (6-13, 15)
don't reduce to a subject-list comparison — some are yes/no, some ask
"why," one wants a citation — so scoring them would mean inventing a
convention `questions.md` never states; this slice scores
`reconcile_agent.py`'s five deterministic answers against the hand-derived
key instead, and leaves the rest recorded as unscored rather than silently
approximated. Every one of the five scores exact, computed from
`test_reconcile_agent.py`'s own real fixture, not a synthetic one. The
Wilson-interval tests are three properties **hand-derived by algebra**,
not pulled from a fetched or half-remembered example — a first attempt
reached for a reference numeric example from Wikipedia's own Wilson-score
article and got back garbled math markup with no worked example at all
(a second source 403'd); deriving three exact boundary identities
(`successes == n` → upper bound exactly `1.0`; `successes == 0` → lower
bound exactly `0.0`; `successes == n/2` → midpoint exactly `0.5`) turned
out to be more rigorous than trusting a recalled example would have been,
and is the same "verify external formats via real web research" discipline
this project has hit before, applied one step further — verify, and when
the source doesn't cooperate, derive instead of guessing. No mutmut
report, for the identical structural reason `105s` already hit and this
slice didn't need to rediscover.
**Extended to 6-11, 13 and 15, 11 August 2026** (`105z`,
`plans/105z-eval-scoring-discrimination.md`) — a correction to the "ten of
fifteen" pessimism above as much as an extension: checked properly
against the answer key, six of those ten (6-11) turned out to score
exactly the same way 1-5 already do, just with the routing table widened
from `(label, text)` to `(text, labels, candidates)` so a question can
ask about one named invoice ("is INV-1001 compliant") rather than every
invoice carrying one label. `reconcile_agent.py` now answers and scores
eleven questions deterministically, all exact against
`test_reconcile_agent.py`'s own real fixture. A new `score_narration()`
(`eval_scoring.py`) extracts invoice mentions from free prose and scores
them the same way, closing the "no scoring convention" gap for 13 and
15 — proven against a scripted model
(`gst_investigation_agent.py --score-all`, wired but not run live), with
one limitation stated as a test rather than discovered later: mention
extraction has no negation awareness, so a narration that names an
invoice specifically to rule it out (question 8's own shape) scores as a
false positive. **Question 12 stays genuinely unscored** — its key names
no invoice at all (a rate and a notification number), so the
invoice-mention convention has nothing honest to check it against; a
citation-matching convention is real, separate work. Today's evaluation
is `reconcile_agent.py`'s eleven deterministic, scored answers plus P11's
worked agent example (Q14) and the wired-but-unrun 13/15 scoring path —
twelve of fifteen questions now have a real automated score, one (12) is
a stated, deliberate gap, and two (12's own citation form aside) await a
live model key.
**P8's date-window traversal shipped, 11 August 2026** (`105u`): the third
gap `105i` named and deliberately did not attempt — the graph walk itself
filtered to entities valid at a date, not only resolving already-fetched
rows. Same predicate-supplied-by-the-caller posture
`relationship_types` already has, so no pack-specific hardcoding; both
`graph-owl-traversal-memory` and `graph-owl-engine-postgres` exclude an
invalid node *before* the walk runs, so it cannot be a pass-through to a
node beyond it (proven, not assumed — a dedicated test). Mutation-clean at
both layers (16/14/2/0 memory; 3/3/0/0 Postgres, the small count because
most of the Postgres change is SQL text with nothing for a mutant to
touch).
**P8 closed the same day** (`105v`): entity-validity extension of
`as_of`/time-travel at the storage layer, applied to SPARQL's own
scoped-facts computation before the evaluator runs, the same position
`as_of` itself already occupies. A dedicated predicate-scoped fetch, not a
read of the pushdown-narrowed dataset — a query never naming
`effectiveFrom`/`effectiveTo` would otherwise silently treat a dated
subject as undated. Found and fixed a real bug this way: pushdown only
fetches what the query pattern names, so the first version of this check
read as "always valid" for exactly the queries most likely to touch dated
data. `105i`'s remaining named gap — a pack-config surface for declaring a
temporal-resolution rule — is still open.

**P9 closed the same day too** (`105w`, `105x`): entity linking
(`Catalog::link_entity`, free text to *any* graph subject `Sid`, not only
a catalog asset — a different problem from `resolve_entity`) and
hybrid-search fusion (`graph_owl_core::hybrid::fuse`, a new pure
lexical/semantic/graph scorer mirroring `recall::rank`'s own architecture
without forcing a non-memory candidate to carry memory-only fields).
`link_entity` fuses lexical similarity with a graph-connectivity term (a
bounded one-hop reach fraction via `TraversalEngine::neighbours`);
`semantic` stays honestly `None` throughout — no graph subject in general
has an embedding today, only memories do (Epic 31's own scope), and
inventing one here would misreport "not measured" as "measured, and
dissimilar." Mutation-clean at both layers after one round of
strengthening (`graph-owl-core/hybrid.rs` 10/11 caught, 1 unviable;
`graph-owl-api` 11/13 caught, 1 unviable, 1 documented-equivalent
tie-break).

**Per-named-graph policy closed 11 August 2026** (`105y`): the exact gap
`105-domain-neutrality.md` named — "a pack's facts are now readable by any
principal who can query... it is not the right long-term answer for a
pack carrying real invoices" — closed with a `NamedGraph` counterpart to
the existing `Fqn` access predicate (`graph-owl-authz`), checked at the
same two `scope_facts` sites that already carve out the three hardcoded
medical vocabularies. `Principal::system()`'s admin bypass means almost
every existing test was unaffected by construction; only a non-admin
principal querying import-sourced pack data is newly gated. The RED test
surfaced an unrelated, already-documented gotcha along the way: a plain
`SELECT` cannot see a fact with a `cx` at all (`FlakeDataset` places it in
a **named** RDF graph, not the default one) — `105-domain-neutrality.md`
already said so ("every pattern must be inside `GRAPH ?g`"), and the first
version of the test hadn't applied it to its own query. Mutation-clean:
`graph-owl-authz` 8/12 caught + 4 unviable; `graph-owl-storage-postgres`'s
`lower()` exhaustiveness arm needed a re-run outside the default
`--lib`-scoped script (that function has no unit-test caller, only
integration ones) — 10/10 caught once scoped correctly.

**GSP/ERP connectors: real code against published specs, already proven —
both the connector and the "why not live this session" story predate this
paragraph.** `connectors/python/graph_owl_packs/gstr2b.py` and `erpnext.py`
are real HTTP clients (`urllib.request`, no mocked transport) against
field names taken from a real Postman collection and a live sandbox, not
recall — normalized through a pure `normalize()`/`to_turtle()` pipeline
so a fixture and a live response are interchangeable to the six finding
rules. 37 tests pass. `105a-gstr2b-provider-behaviour.md` is the record of
an earlier live-sandbox investigation this same day: two independent GSPs
tested, and GSTR-2B API access turned out to be a **provisioned, paid
capability at both** (`AUTH4037` / a byte-identical canned mock) — not a
connector defect. The practical acquisition path the market actually
runs on, and what this connector already implements, is the taxpayer's
own portal JSON download fed through `from_file()`; the GSP API fetch
path is a scheduling convenience on top of that, still blocked
externally, not a missing capability. This session deliberately did not
repeat that live testing — asked directly, given `gstcreds.md`/`.env`
also being pointed at, and the answer was "format reference only," so
`fetch()`'s real HTTP path stays unexercised against a live host this
session, same as before.

Still open: six of the seven planned packs are unwritten (deliberately
not attempted — DN-3's hospitality pack already carries the
domain-neutrality proof, and inventing regulatory content for six more
domains without real source material was assessed and declined); and
P2's remaining connectors, P3, P4 are untouched — this is the spine, not
the finished platform

### Console half
- [x] **105** **One queue, every pack** — `findingsQueue.tsx` renders GST and hospitality identically; the difference lives in `pack.toml`. A `GstFindingsQueue.tsx` would have been the console's first per-domain hardcoding
- [x] **105e/105g** **Evidence graph, rendered** — a real Cytoscape-rendered picture plus a raw triples table (extracted `GraphCanvas` out of `App.tsx` for reuse), each node's source document(s) shown as tags, and — for `GstinTransposition` specifically — a "Possible match — not linked" section naming the second Supplier candidate, deliberately not drawn as a graph edge
- [x] **105h / F4** **Obligation calendar — the console's ninth route** — due date first, a colored status tag on the derived "Due" column that the asserted "Anchor" column never carries, `?pack=`/`?window=` deep links
- [x] **F1** **Pack management admin tab — shipped 11 August 2026** (`plans/105-f1-pack-admin-tab.md`). `PackImportPanel.tsx` answered "how do I load data into a pack"; nothing answered "which packs are installed" or let an admin move to what a pack has without navigating there by hand. New "Packs" tab in Admin: browses every installed pack via a new `installedPacks()` — deliberately not a reuse of `surfacesFor()`, which filters to packs with an upload surface and would hide one registered only through `graph-owl-load-pack` — plus a **Reconcile now** action and a **View obligations** action that switches the console to Obligations scoped to that pack (`setObligationCalendarParams` — exported since the obligation calendar shipped, never called until now). Verified against a real running demo, not just unit tests: signed in with `./scripts/demo.sh --secure`'s own printed token, loaded the GST pack for real, clicked **Reconcile now** and confirmed a real `POST /packs/gst/reconcile` (200) in the network panel, clicked **View obligations** and watched the URL become `?section=obligations&pack=gst` with two real obligation rows rendering. Found, not fixed, along the way: the obligation calendar's own default of `readParam("pack") ?? "gst"` would silently point a hospitality-only deployment at a pack that does not exist for them — recorded in the plan rather than folded into this slice.
- [x] **F1 follow-up** **One place for packs, contents made visible, install from the console — shipped 13 August 2026.** Direct user complaint: packs were split across this tab (browse/switch/reconcile) and Connectors' own `PackImportPanel` (upload), and nothing in the UI answered "what did installing this pack actually give me." `PackImportPanel.tsx` is deleted; its upload logic merged into `PackAdminPanel.tsx` behind antd `Table`'s `expandable` row — collapsed by default, so browsing several installed packs still costs one glance, not a scroll past every upload surface (the same goal the original split existed for, solved differently). Expanding a row shows **"What's inside this pack"**, assembled from data that was already real and already admin-gated but never consumed by any client: `GET /packs/{pack}/finding-rules` for the findings it can detect (label, summary, the provision it cites) and `GET /ontology-packs` for the glossary it imported (term count, version) — each section ends in a `→` pointer to where in the console the real thing lives (Review tab, Vocabulary → Glossary, Obligations tab), because "what did this even do" was the concrete question being answered, not a second copy of `pack.toml` re-typed into TypeScript. The GSTR-2B upload surface now carries the exact "Where to get it" guidance text supplied verbatim (GST portal → Returns Dashboard → period → GSTR-2B → Download JSON; a GSP API response is the same document). New **"Available to install"** section below the table lists packs found on disk but not yet installed (`GET /packs/available`, server-side `pack.toml` header scan via the newly-adopted `toml` crate — MIT OR Apache-2.0, checked against `plans/00l-build-vs-adopt.md`'s gate before use) with a real **Install** button per pack (`POST /packs/{pack}/install`). Installing does not reimplement `pack.toml`'s grammar a second time in Rust: the route shells out to the same already-tested Python `graph-owl-load-pack` CLI an operator would run by hand, the same "separate process, not reimplemented" boundary `agent_service` already draws (`plans/00j-language-boundaries.md`) — and forwards the *caller's own* bearer token (a new `RawToken` extractor) into the subprocess rather than a shared service credential, so the install is genuinely attributed to whoever clicked. `pack_id_is_safe()` refuses a path-traversal-shaped id before it is ever joined onto a filesystem path (tested against a URL-encoded `../../../etc`). Live-verified end to end against the real running stack, not just the 7 new integration tests and 6 new unit tests: Connectors no longer renders any pack content; expanding the installed GST row shows all six real finding rules and `20 term(s), version 1`; clicking **Install** on `hospitality` triggered the real 5-call loader sequence in the server log (`/namespaces` → 6×`/predicates` → 2×`/graph/import/rdf` → `POST /packs/hospitality/install` 200 in 127ms, attributed to the real signed-in principal) and the pack moved live from "Available to install" into the table with namespace `1025`; expanding *its* row correctly shows the honest empty state ("This pack registers no finding rules" / "no glossary" / "no upload surface — its data lands only via its own bundled fixtures at install time") rather than fabricating content for a pack shaped differently from GST. Scoping note recorded rather than hidden: the install route's own integration test cannot exercise a full install through this project's `oneshot()`-based test harness (no real bound port for the subprocess to call back into), so it proves the spawn/argument/output-capture machinery against a refused port instead — the loader's own HTTP behaviour is `connectors/python/tests/`' coverage, not re-derived here.

### Plan 106 — agent trace hygiene, shipped 12 August 2026
Triggered by reviewing a live LangSmith trace of the GST investigation agent (`plans/106-agent-trace-hygiene.md`), four independent gaps, four slices, four commits — `plans/105-mcp-tool-visibility-divergence.md` is the investigation that dissected the visibility thread this plan executes the follow-ups for.
- [x] **Slice 1** LangSmith env hygiene (`b70535c`) — `.env.example` aligned to the var name the installed SDK actually reads, docstring fixed.
- [x] **Slice 2** `query_graph` diagnostics (`5e07c23`) — `QueryAnswer` gained `factsScanned`/`plan`/`variables`/`qlRewrite`/`refusedAxioms`/`alignmentsUsed`, populated from `SparqlOutcome`, which already carried them but the MCP handler dropped all but `rows`/`truncated`. In the trace this was the worst silent moment: `governedBy` came back `[]` with no way to tell the law graph was never scanned.
- [x] **Slice 3a** honest asset-scope reason (`5e07c23`) — D1 = keep `recall_memory`/`record_investigation` asset-scoped by design, not extended to pack subjects. `Sid::is_runtime_pack_namespace` (zero-DB IRI check) lets `recall_memory` answer a pack subject with "memory is scoped to catalog assets; pack entities are queried via query_graph" instead of the generic, ambiguous "not visible to you" string every other refusal shares.
- [x] **Slice 3b** `explain` over pack named graphs (`0dfbfcd`) — D2 = extend. `explain_fact` resolves a subject's own source graph(s) and widens `Budget.include_graphs` before reasoning; previously every pack-fact `explain` was `NotFound` regardless of principal because the reasoner was never shown the graph the fact lives in. Bounded to the subject's own graphs — a regression the slice's own test caught: naively widening would let a subject's own prior conclusion in `graph:reasoning` feed back into a "fresh" explanation as if asserted, so that graph is explicitly excluded.
- [x] **Slices 4a+4b** `provision-in-force` becomes answerable (`c67f29e`) — D3 = named parameterized pack query, the concrete fix for the trace's actual failed question: `gst:governedBy` is declared but never instantiated, so the agent answered a 10% cap question by in-context arithmetic instead of a graph fact. **4a**: a new `PackQueryRegistry` (Postgres, mirrors `FindingRuleRegistry`) plus `{{name}}` placeholder substitution — syntax-level only, always inside a query author's own `<...>`, validated against SPARQL 1.1's real `IRIREF` grammar rather than an invented blocklist. `packs/gst/queries/provision-in-force.sparql` registered, resolving the same `OPTIONAL` + `!BOUND` "latest `effectiveFrom` on or before the date" idiom `amount-mismatch.sparql` already uses. **4b**: `run_pack_query(pack, name, bindings)`, the ninth MCP tool, going through `Catalog::sparql` directly so it inherits Slice 2's diagnostics and Slice 3b's named-graph widening for free; not admin-gated, matching `query_graph`'s read-only posture. Mutation testing found and fixed a real gap in `placeholder_names`'s offset arithmetic (adjacent placeholders with no separating text — a mutant `end * 2` for `end + 2` silently dropped the second placeholder from `declared`); the sibling `declare_pack_query -> Ok(())` survivor was the known `cargo-mutants -p`-scoping blind spot (Slice 2 hit the same one), confirmed caught by hand-applying the exact mutant against the real `graph-owl-server` integration suite (3 of 4 tests failed with 404s) rather than accepted on faith.

### The console gets a chat tab — shipped 12 August 2026, a deliberate reversal
- [x] **Agent tab, embedded in the shipped console.** A direct, explicit user decision to reverse the earlier "keep the chat playground outside the shipped product" call — `plans/00j-language-boundaries.md`'s "never a hosted agent runtime" position still holds in the sense that matters: the agent runtime is still a separate Python/LangGraph process (`integrations/langchain/agent_service/`, promoted out of `examples/` for this), never ported into `graph-owl-server`. What changed is that the console's React frontend is now a direct HTTP/SSE *consumer* of that service, the same relationship it already has with graph-owl-server itself — not the agent runtime becoming part of the engine. New sidebar item ("Agent", after Admin), `ui/src/features/agentChat/`, wired through `App.tsx`'s existing `?section=` mechanism the same way every other route is (and, while touching that exact whitelist, fixed a real pre-existing gap: `"obligations"` was missing from the initial-param-read list, so a cold load on `?section=obligations` silently fell through to Overview — included both it and `"agent"` explicitly rather than repeating the gap).
- [x] **Per-request auth, not a shared service token — the actual point of embedding this in the console.** `agent_service/server.py`'s `POST /questions` now reads the caller's own `Authorization: Bearer` header and threads it through to that question's `GraphOwlToolkit`/`Principal`, so an investigation run from the console sees exactly what its signed-in user is authorized to see. TDD: `test_agent_service_server.py` runs a real `uvicorn` server (not `TestClient`, which drops the fire-and-forget background task between separate calls — found live) and proves two concurrent questions with different caller tokens each build their own toolkit with the right token, not a shared one.
- [x] **The "ask a second question while the first still runs" property, proven twice** — first as the standalone `agent_service/static/index.html` page (screenshot-verified, real DeepSeek model, real GST data), then again as the React panel: two questions submitted back to back in the real embedded console, both showed the running-status dot simultaneously, both completed with their own distinct answers, switching between them in the sidebar correctly preserves each one's own text. Zero console errors both times.
- [x] **A real bug found live, not in a test fixture**: the first real-model run showed a broad `query_graph` tool call's raw JSON result rendering directly in the chat as if it were the model's own prose. Root cause: LangGraph's `"messages"` stream mode forwards every message flowing through the graph, including `ToolMessage` (a tool's own return value, which has a `.content` attribute exactly like `AIMessageChunk` does) — not only AI-generated text. Fixed with `isinstance(chunk, AIMessage)`; TDD'd RED (via `git stash` on just the one file, confirming the existing scripted-model test's small tool result already silently leaked) then GREEN.
- [x] **A second, separate real finding, left open on purpose**: the configured `LLM_MODEL` is a DeepSeek reasoning ("thinking mode") model, and there is a currently-open upstream bug in `langchain-deepseek` (`langchain-ai/langchain` issues #34166, #37174) — `ChatDeepSeek._get_request_payload()` drops the `reasoning_content` field DeepSeek's API requires echoed back after any turn that made a tool call, so a real multi-hop investigation 400s on its second model call. Not fixable in this repo; documented rather than silently worked around. Toolless questions (used for the live verification above) sidestep it entirely.
- [x] **Agent selector is real infrastructure for one entry, not a stub.** `AGENTS` in `AgentChat.tsx` is a plain list; the picker, the request payload, and per-thread state already key off `agentId`, so a second agent is a one-line addition, not a redesign — the user's own stated requirement ("allow me to select the different agents") ahead of a second agent actually existing.
- [x] **Bundle budget checked, not assumed** — `npm run check:budgets` was already over its 350KB gzip budget before this change (620.4KB, pre-existing, confirmed via `git stash`); this feature adds ~2KB to that, not the violation.
- [x] **Real per-token streaming and tool-call visibility, replacing `create_agent` — found and fixed 12 August 2026.** User-reported: text appeared to "jump" to the final answer rather than growing token by token, the opposite of Cursor/Claude/ChatGPT's own chat UX. Two separate causes, both fixed: (1) `create_agent`'s model node (`langchain/agents/factory.py::_execute_model_async`) always calls `model.ainvoke(...)`, never `.astream(...)` — whether any deltas reach the caller at all depends on LangChain's callback-triggered auto-streaming kicking in for the specific provider, which it did not for this deployment's DeepSeek endpoint. Fixed by replacing `create_agent` with a hand-rolled ReAct loop (`run_investigation_stream` in `streaming.py`) that calls `model.bind_tools(tools).astream(messages)` directly, merges `AIMessageChunk`s as they arrive, and yields each delta immediately — proven with a new scripted model that implements real `_stream`/`_astream` (the previous scripted model only exercised `BaseChatModel`'s single-blob fallback, which cannot distinguish "streams" from "doesn't"). (2) Both frontends (`AgentChat.tsx` and the standalone `static/index.html`) were **replacing** displayed text with each incoming delta instead of appending it — so even a model that streamed correctly only ever showed its last-arrived chunk. Fixed both to append. The rewrite also adds Cursor/Claude/ChatGPT-style tool-call badges ("Using `search_assets`…" → "✓ Used `search_assets`") via a new `"update"` SSE event carrying only `{phase, tool, ok}` — never the tool's raw output, which was a second real bug this same work found live (a `query_graph` call's raw JSON rendering directly in the chat transcript). **A third live-found bug**: two consecutive model turns' prose concatenated with no separator at all ("...tables relatedThe catalog has 15 tables..."); fixed by yielding a paragraph break before the first content chunk of any turn after the first. Verified end to end in the browser against the real demo server: a real multi-hop investigation (7 tool calls) streamed visibly, badge by badge, with correctly-separated paragraphs and a complete, well-formatted final answer — the DeepSeek `reasoning_content` limitation noted above did not surface on this run, though it remains a real, open upstream bug for sufficiently long investigations.
- [x] **A related, separate gap in the demo script itself, found the same session**: `agent_service` has no `.env` loading of its own — it inherits whatever the launching shell exported, which is easy to get wrong (the standalone dev-tool origin of this service meant this was never load-bearing until the console started depending on it). Documented rather than silently special-cased: `agent_service/README.md`'s startup instructions now say explicitly to source the repo root `.env` before launching uvicorn. Separately, when the console runs with OIDC off (`OIDC_ISSUER=`), there is no signed-in access token for the browser to forward, so `GRAPH_OWL_TOKEN` must be set as agent_service's fallback even though graph-owl-server itself ignores the token's value in open mode (every request resolves to the system principal) — the 401 this produces without it is agent_service's own auth check, not graph-owl-server's.

### Agent file upload + reasoning_content fallback + rotating "thinking" labels — shipped 12 August 2026
- [x] **The DeepSeek `reasoning_content` 400 fixed for real, not just documented.** The prior entry above left this as an open upstream bug (`langchain-ai/langchain` #34166/#37174: `ChatOpenAI` never round-trips a reasoning model's `reasoning_content` field, so a request echoing back a prior tool-calling turn 400s). The user hit it live. Fix: `run_investigation_stream` (`streaming.py`) now catches that *specific, narrow* error (substring-matched, not any 400) and retries the same turn against a second, configured `LLM_FALLBACK_MODEL` — reusing the exact convention `reconcile_agent.py`'s own `narrate()` already established for a reasoning model that stalls instead of errors, just applied to a different failure mode. Guarded on four conditions so this can never become a silent "swallow every failure" path: a fallback must be configured, this turn must not have already tried it, the model must not already be the fallback, and no content may have arrived yet this attempt (a 400 arrives before any stream chunk, so a mid-stream failure is a different, real problem and is never retried). A `model_fallback` update event tells the UI what happened, rendered as a visible amber notice — not silently switched underneath the user. TDD: a scripted model that raises the exact error string, a scripted model that raises an unrelated error (proves the narrow match — no fallback triggered), and a no-fallback-configured case (proves the default behaviour is unchanged).
- [x] **Rotating, coloured "thinking" labels, the way Cursor/Claude/ChatGPT do it.** The static `"…"` placeholder is replaced with a 10-word rotating list ("Thinking", "Cogitating", "Catapulating", "Percolating", "Ruminating", "Noodling", "Synthesizing", "Pondering", "Untangling", "Marinating") cycling every 1.4s while a thread has no text or tool activity yet, rendered with a moving gradient-shimmer sweep (`base.css`'s `.gowl-thinking-label`, the product's own `#14C3CF` accent either side of a lighter highlight) rather than plain text. Verified live: two separate questions showed two different words ("Cogitating…" then "Catapulating…"), confirming the rotation runs independently per thread rather than sharing one global timer.
- [x] **File upload + preview + a real ad hoc reconciliation tool, the actual feature requested.** A user can now attach a file (paperclip button in the composer) to a question — `agent_service` gained `POST /files`/`GET /files/{id}` (in-memory, session-scoped, the same posture `_THREADS` already has), and the console shows staged file chips before sending, attached-file chips on the sent thread, and a click-to-preview modal (pretty-printed JSON via `JSON.parse`/`JSON.stringify`, raw text as a fallback for anything else) sourced from the exact same `GET /files/{id}` a tool call would read. When at least one file is attached, the agent gains a ninth-ish tool, `reconcile_uploaded_files(gstr2b_file_id, purchase_register_file_id)` (`agent_service/reconcile_uploaded.py`) — a **pure, TDD'd, I/O-free** invoice-matching function (`compare_purchase_records`, 13 tests: boundary at the exact ₹1 rounding tolerance, a genuine-vs-transposed GSTIN negative pair, priority ordering when cancelled-and-amount-mismatched are both true) wrapped in a thin LangChain `@tool`. **Deliberately not the same mechanism as the pack-backed "Run reconciliation" button already in Governance** — no graph write, no persisted finding, a fixed rounding tolerance rather than Rule 36(4)'s legally-derived, date-sensitive cap — see `reconcile_uploaded.py`'s own docstring for the full distinction, stated up front rather than left to be discovered.
- [x] **Live-verified against a hand-derived answer key, not eyeballed.** Two sample files (`agent_service/sample_data/`, 11 invoices, one planted in each of the tool's 8 outcome categories) were run through `compare_purchase_records` directly *before* any live question, producing an answer key. Two real questions against the real running console then matched it exactly — 11/11 invoices correctly categorised on "reconcile these files," and a second, harder question ("which invoices carry real ITC risk") correctly separated inadmissible-credit invoices from merely-correctable ones from a legitimate-but-unclaimed one, real domain reasoning rather than a template restatement. Full transcript and answer key: `plans/agent-file-upload-results.md`.
- [x] **A real limitation found live, left in the writeup rather than smoothed over**: a badly-scoped third question ("...the reconciliation you just ran...") in a **new** thread with no files re-attached sent the agent on a 27+-tool-call unproductive search through the real GST pack graph, because threads share no context by design (`streaming.py`'s own architecture — independent investigations, not a continuous conversation). Not a bug; a correct demonstration of thread isolation surfaced by a question that assumed ChatGPT-style cross-turn memory. Documented as a real usage caveat: re-attach files (or restate fully) on every new question.

### Agent chat unmount bug + demo data-loss root cause — fixed 12 August 2026
- [x] **`AgentChat` was unmounted, not just hidden, on every section switch — App.tsx's own routing pattern, not a rendering accident.** Every section (`Overview`, `Governance`, … `Agent`) rendered inside one ternary chain inside a single `<Content>`, so navigating away destroyed the whole component instance — including `streamsRef`'s live `EventSource` connections, whose cleanup effect closes every open stream on unmount. Switching tabs mid-investigation silently killed it; switching back showed "No questions yet" as if it had never been asked, which is what the user meant by "history isn't retained." **Fix**: `AgentChat` now renders in a second, permanently-mounted `<Content>` sibling, toggled with `display: none`/`undefined` instead of being conditionally rendered — every other section's ternary branch is untouched. Verified live with real DOM clicks (`element.click()`, not the browser-automation tool's ref-based click — see below) on the left-nav menu: asked a real question, navigated to Governance, navigated back to Agent, the thread and its (429-rate-limited) error state were still there.
- [x] **Found live: the browser-automation tool's ref-based `left_click` on the left-nav `<Menu>` item was silently forcing a full page reload**, not a real click — `find()` → `left_click(ref)` on "Governance" fetched fresh JS/CSS/fonts and re-ran the Auth0 token exchange every time, which looks exactly like "the SPA does a hard navigation on every tab switch" until checked directly. A raw `document.querySelector('[role="menuitem"]').click()` via the JS tool on the identical element produced zero document-level requests — only the page's own data fetch. Recorded because it produced a wrong diagnosis first (nearly concluded the app's own routing was broken) before the cheap, direct check (`read_network_requests` immediately before/after each click style) caught it.
- [x] **Root cause of "packs/vocabulary/governance/agent chat all look empty" was a genuine data wipe, not a UI bug** — `scripts/demo.sh` unconditionally runs `docker rm -f ${CONTAINER}` on every invocation (confirmed by reading the script, not assumed), so a `./scripts/demo.sh --gst` run with real OIDC active — which the user ran directly, per their own pasted terminal output — recreated Postgres from scratch and then failed the auto-catalogue and GST-pack-load steps for lack of a token, leaving an empty, OIDC-authenticated server running. A prior session's fully-seeded database (from an `OIDC_ISSUER= OIDC_AUDIENCE=` demo run) was silently gone. **Fix applied**: re-ran the seed pipeline once more (`OIDC_ISSUER= OIDC_AUDIENCE= ./scripts/demo.sh --gst`, which the demo script itself already recommends when OIDC-token acquisition fails), then restarted the `graph-owl-server` **binary directly** — not `demo.sh` — with no OIDC override, letting it load real `OIDC_ISSUER`/`OIDC_AUDIENCE` from `.env` via `dotenvy` while pointed at the now-seeded Postgres container. Confirmed via a direct `psql` query (bypassing HTTP auth entirely) that 124 assets, the `pack:gst` namespace, and 9 findings survived the OIDC flip. **Standing gotcha for next time**: `demo.sh` is not idempotent — re-running it against a deployment you want to keep discards it. To restart without losing data, restart the `graph-owl-server` binary directly against the existing `DATABASE_URL`; never re-run `demo.sh` itself once real data exists.
- [x] **Governance's "Run validation"/"Run reasoning" and the Packs/Vocabulary tabs were never broken** — confirmed live once real data existed: `Run validation` → "0 shape(s) ran, nothing violated" (the GST pack registers no SHACL shapes, a correct zero, not a failure), `Run reasoning` → "Full run — 0 derived, 0 replaced, maintained to t=184" (no OWL entailment rules in this pack, likewise a correct zero), `Admin → Packs` shows the GST pack once its `pack:gst`-declared namespace exists, `Vocabulary → Glossary/Ontology packs` shows the imported `GstReconciliation` term. All four needed only the data and, for Governance, an actual click — both buttons work end to end.
- [x] **`Admin → Agent activity` is empty by design, not a bug** — it lists real `PUT /agents/{id}/grant` grants and the MCP tool-call history attributed to that granted identity (Epic 42 Slice F). The Reconciliation Agent (`agent_service`) deliberately has no such identity: per task "Agent service: per-request auth," every MCP call it makes runs under the *signed-in user's own* forwarded OIDC token, precisely so the chat agent can never see or do more than the person asking it questions. That design choice is what leaves this ledger empty for chat-originated activity — a real architectural gap between "delegated-auth chat agent" and "autonomously-granted agent identity," not something to silently paper over by granting the agent its own elevated identity.

### Model provider picker (Ollama + opencode) + full question text in the transcript — shipped 12 August 2026
- [x] **The opencode.ai/zen free tier hit a real, external `FreeUsageLimitError` 429** — confirmed live: a fresh API key made no difference (restarted `agent_service` to force the reload, identical error), which is the tell that `-free`-suffixed models on that gateway are a shared pool, not a per-key quota. Not fixable from this codebase; the fix is picking a different model.
- [x] **`agent_service.providers`** (new, TDD, 10 unit tests + 3 server-integration tests) — a small provider/model registry, not a hardcoded list: `"opencode"` reflects whatever `LLM_MODEL`/`LLM_FALLBACK_MODEL`/`LLM_API_BASE_URL` are actually set to; `"ollama"` queries a locally-running Ollama server's own `GET /api/tags` live, listing both local models and Ollama's own cloud-proxied `*:cloud` models exactly as Ollama itself reports them — unreachable or unconfigured, a provider is simply absent from the list, not shown as a dead option. `GET /providers` (new route) serves this to the console. `POST /questions` accepts optional `provider`/`model` fields; `_run_and_publish` resolves them through `resolve_model`/`build_chat_model_from` instead of the default env-based `build_chat_model()` — proven by a test with a poisoned default model that must never be called when a provider is explicitly selected. No fallback-model retry applies to an explicit pick (that retry is specifically the default path's own known `reasoning_content` bug, not a general safety net).
- [x] **Console**: two new `<Select>`s in the Agent tab (originally the sidebar — see the redesign immediately below, which moved them into the composer the same day), provider then model, fetched from `GET /providers` on mount and defaulting to the first of each. `askQuestion` threads the selection through unchanged when nothing is picked (an unreachable agent service still behaves exactly as before the picker existed).
- [x] **Live-verified with real questions against a real local Ollama** (10 models detected: `qwen3.6:latest`/`qwen3.6:27b-mlx` local, `gpt-oss:20b-cloud`/`gemma4:cloud`/`kimi-k2.6:cloud`/`deepseek-v4-flash:cloud`/`qwen3.5:cloud`/`deepseek-v3.2:cloud`/`glm-5.1:cloud` proxied through Ollama's own cloud, plus `nomic-embed-text:latest`): one question against `gpt-oss:20b-cloud` and one against the genuinely local `qwen3.6:latest`, both answered correctly through the exact same picker.
- [x] **Fixed: the user's own question was only ever visible truncated in the sidebar, never in full in the transcript** — like every other chat UI, the full question now renders as its own block at the top of the transcript pane (`AgentChat.tsx`), confirmed live alongside the provider-picker questions above.

### Agent chat redesign: Markdown answers, composer-integrated controls, history popup — shipped 13 August 2026
- [x] **Answers render as real Markdown, not raw text.** The `***`/unrendered-table complaint was exactly what it looked like: `active.text` went straight into a plain `<Text>`. Now wrapped in `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` (both already present in `package.json` when this slice started) inside a new `.gowl-markdown` class (`base.css`) styling tables, bold, headings, code, blockquotes and lists to match the product rather than rendering unstyled browser defaults — theme-agnostic `rgba` overlays, the same approach `.gowl-row-cursor` already uses, so no separate dark/light variant was needed. A wide table scrolls inside itself (`display: block; overflow-x: auto`) rather than forcing the transcript pane wider.
- [x] **Sidebar removed; the transcript gets the width back.** The old 280px `Sider` (title, intro, three selects, thread list) is gone. Title + intro now sit in a slim header row at the top of the single full-width `Content` pane, with a `History` button on the same row, top-right.
- [x] **Agent picker and the provider/model picker both moved into the composer**, beside the paperclip attach button — the same row as the text input, not a separate panel. Provider and model are now **one merged, grouped `<Select>`** (`"<providerId>::<modelId>"` as the value, split/joined at its one call site) rather than two separate controls, with each provider rendered as a group label and its models as the options beneath it — confirmed live: the same dropdown lists `opencode.ai/zen`'s two models and `Ollama (local)`'s ten in one list.
- [x] **Question history moved from a permanent sidebar list into a popup.** The `History` button opens a `Modal` listing every thread (status dot + question); clicking one sets it active and closes the popup — the exact same `threadEntries` data the old sidebar list rendered, just relocated.
- [x] **Live-verified end to end with the user's own reported question**, attached files and all: `gstr2b_sample.json` + `purchase_register_sample.json` uploaded via the composer's attach button, provider/model switched to `Ollama (local)` → `gpt-oss:20b-cloud` (opencode.ai/zen was still 429'd at test time), asked *"Reconcile these two attached files and tell me every mismatch, grouped by category."* — rendered as a real Markdown table (Category / Invoice(s) / Brief Detail columns, bold category names, monospace invoice numbers and GSTINs), all 7 mismatch categories and 4 matches correct against the same answer key `plans/agent-file-upload-results.md` already established, tool badge `Used reconcile_uploaded_files` shown, `History` popup opening and loading the thread confirmed in the same session.

### Plan 108 — Books ↔ GSTR-1 ↔ GSTR-2B, and a workspace a CA works down (shipped 13 August 2026)

- [x] **GSTR-1/GSTR-2A as a third evidence source, entirely as pack content** — `gst:Gstr1Invoice`, `gst:filedDate`, `gst:GoodsReceipt` and a genuinely new `gst:Section16-2-b` in `law/sections.ttl`; no Rust, no loader change, no new console component. Until now "not in your GSTR-2B" was one finding with two causes needing opposite next actions: the supplier never filed it, or they filed it and it never reached this taxpayer. Five new rules now tell them apart — `SupplierNotFiled`, `Gstr1NotIn2b` (carrying the supplier's own filing date, which is usually the whole explanation), `MissingInBooks` (the reverse direction, and the one finding here that can be worth money rather than cost it), `BooksGstr1Mismatch` (same Rule 36(4) cap logic, different named graph) and `GoodsReceiptTiming` (the one rule that is not a document comparison at all — every document agrees and the goods had not arrived). `gst:PotentialMismatch` deliberately stands down once any GSTR-1 evidence exists, with a **global** rather than per-invoice guard: a per-invoice one would leave it firing on exactly `SupplierNotFiled`'s population, the same accusation twice in two vocabularies. The verify script asserts the handover in both directions, not only that the new rules fire.
- [x] **Upload your own books and GSTR-2A, not just GSTR-2B** — `books.ts` reads a purchase register as CSV/TSV, which is the file a CA actually has; before this the taxpayer's own books could arrive *only* as a Turtle fixture shipped inside the pack, so a real GSTR-2B was being reconciled against somebody else's demo data. Column names are matched on a normalized alias because they are not standardisable (the practitioner formats use `GST NO.`/`PARTICULARS`/`INV. NO`/`BASIC`, an ERP export says `Supplier GSTIN`/`Voucher No`/`Taxable Value`). A missing required column **stops** the import and names itself rather than importing a subset: matching is GSTIN + invoice number, so a register without a GSTIN column joins to nothing and produces a page of confident, entirely wrong findings. `gstr1.ts` reads a GSTR-2A download — what a recipient can actually obtain — with field names verified against a published GSP/GSTN reference rather than recalled, which caught three traps a guess gets wrong (`fldtr1`/`flprdr1` live on the supplier block not the invoice, they use `DD-Mon-YY`/`Mon-YY` where invoice dates are `DD-MM-YYYY`, and taxable value is per line item so a multi-rate invoice must be summed). Shared normalizers extracted to `gstText.ts` rather than copied a third time — two `money()`s disagreeing in the last decimal produce mismatches that are not there, with nothing in the output to say which importer was wrong.
- [x] **A Reconciliation route — the console's eleventh — that is the workflow rather than administration.** The Review queue is a reviewer's tool: one finding, its rule, its evidence chain. It is not how a period is closed, which previously meant uploading through an admin table, clicking a button inside one of its rows, reading a flat list in a third section, and doing the arithmetic by hand. The new page is the four steps in order: load the three sources (each showing count, totals and the periods it covers), run the rules, read the statement, work the exceptions, download the working paper. The statement reports what a spreadsheet cannot — books total, GSTR-2B total, the difference, how much the findings explain, and **how much they do not**, naming the invoices the residual consists of. A statement that always balances means nothing, so the residual is surfaced rather than absorbed. Each finding renders as *what to do about it*, since a rule's own summary is written for a reviewer of the rule and a CA needs the next action.
- [x] **Verified in a real browser against real Postgres, uploading real-shaped files — and that is where six defects were found that the whole test suite could not see.** All of them passed `tsc`, `eslint` and 700 unit tests. `POST /sparql` returns N-Triples, so every value arrived quoted: `Number('"18000.00"')` is `NaN` and `'"INV-1001"'` joins to nothing, which rendered a column of `₹NaN` *and* silently valued every reconciling line at zero. Slash-containing invoice numbers — `RST/2026/0455`, the ordinary Indian format — produced invalid Turtle and the server rejected whole imports with "1 field failed validation"; all three importers had it, including the GSTR-2B one that shipped months earlier and would have failed on any real return. An `OPTIONAL` supplier-name join fanned out across named graphs and inflated every total by ~44% (26 invoices where the graph held 18, ₹4,82,930 where it held ₹3,06,710 — every figure plausible and every figure false). A sign-per-rule model hard-coded the mismatch rules to explain nothing, so real value differences the rules *had* found were reported as unaccounted for directly below the findings that accounted for them. `PaymentOverdue` projects `gst:atTime` twice and `GoodsReceiptTiming` once, so rendering by predicate name labelled a purchase date "received" — the page asserting a false fact about a tax position. And the finding card showed the statement contribution where a reviewer needs credit at stake, so `₹0.00` sat beside "reverse this credit".
- [x] **A false accusation the fixtures alone would never have shown** — running the pack live surfaced `SupplierNotFiled` firing on INV-1004, whose GSTIN is transposed between the register and the filing. The supplier *had* filed it; an exact join simply could not see it. One typo was producing three findings, two of them wrong, next to the `GstinTransposition` finding already saying the true thing. Both absence rules now stand down when the other side holds the same invoice number at the same taxable value under any GSTIN, and the cost of that guard is written down rather than left to be discovered.
- [x] **End-to-end proof on uploaded data, not fixtures**: a practitioner-format CSV (currency-formatted figures, a trailing totals row, slash-bearing invoice numbers) plus GSTR-2A and GSTR-2B JSON, giving 18/16/14 invoices matching the graph exactly and books ₹3,06,710 hand-checked against the file. All four planted findings fired — `SupplierNotFiled` on SE/JUL/119, `Gstr1NotIn2b` on PL-8901, `MissingInBooks` on TW-4471, `BooksGstr1Mismatch` on RST/2026/0512 — while the reverse-charge invoice KFC-2026-77 correctly produced no finding *and* correctly appeared in the residual, so the money is never hidden even where the rule is right to stay silent. `GET /findings/{id}/evidence-graph` on the new `Gstr1NotIn2b` finding returns the three-way story with per-node provenance: `pr-PL-8901` from `gst-books-2026-07`, `g1-PL-8901` from `gst-gstr1-2026-07`, and no `2b-PL-8901` at all — the absence *is* the finding. All 11 rules are registered, so the agent's generic `run_rule`/`reconcile`/`explain`/`find_evidence` tools answer for the five new ones with no agent-side change.

- [x] **Found and recorded, not silently fixed: the CI-enforced initial-JS-bundle budget (350KB gzipped, `ui/scripts/check-budgets.mjs`) was already failing before this slice touched anything** — measured directly by stashing this slice's changes and rebuilding the prior commit: 624.6KB gzipped, already 274.6KB over budget. `react-markdown`/`remark-gfm` add a further ~47KB gzipped on top (671.8KB final). Fixing the pre-existing gap means code-splitting the app (lazy-loaded routes — the budget doc's own prescribed fix), which is unrelated in scope to a chat-UI redesign and was not attempted here; flagged rather than absorbed into this change.
- [x] **The composer's agent and provider/model selectors became icon-only, on request the same day** — the visible-text `<Select>`s (`Reconciliation Agent`, `deepseek-v4-flash-free`) took real estate the composer didn't need to spend; both are now a plain icon `Button` (`RobotOutlined`/`ApiOutlined`) with a native `title` tooltip (`"Select the agent"`/`"Select the model"`, the same `title`-attribute convention the paperclip attach button already used) wrapped in an antd `Dropdown` — click opens the same agent list / provider-grouped model list as before, `menu.selectedKeys` highlights the current pick. **Verified working despite an automation-only false alarm**: an automated click briefly leaves the popup at `opacity:0` mid-`rc-motion` "prepare" frame (no console error, correct DOM position/content/z-index) — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that a longer wait reaches `opacity:1` before the menu naturally closes again, proving the stall was the automated click racing the enter animation, not a rendering defect a real mouse click would ever hit.
