## Demo 10 — Standards depth

**The claim**: the standards alignment survives inspection by someone who knows the standards.

**What you can show**: export a lineage edge and point at `rdf:reifies << … >>` in the Turtle — then run the *same* query with the standard vocabulary in the workbench and get rows back, not zero. Add an Arabic-labelled term and watch it render right-to-left in the entity header, the search results and the graph node. Author a constraint in SPARQL rather than in the shape language and see it land in the same violations queue.

### Epic 94 — RDF 1.2 alignment — **shipped, 9 August 2026**
- [x] **A** `FlakeValue::TripleTerm` at discriminant 10, pinning test extended
- [x] **B** `rdf:reifies` + triple term on export; store flake count unchanged
- [x] **C** `rdf:dirLangString` — shipped as a new `FlakeValue::LangString` variant, a deliberate deviation from the plan's original `flake_meta` side-table design (recorded in the plan's own decision log)
- [x] **C (console) — canvas-rendered graph-node captions, closed 9 August 2026.** DOM cases (entity header, description, search results, memory content) already rendered `dir` correctly via `userTextDir`; the gap was Cytoscape's `<canvas>` labels, which have no `dir` attribute to hook. New `ui/src/graph/bidiLabel.ts` (`canvasLabel`, MIT `bidi-js` dependency) shapes a label before it reaches Cytoscape in both graph panes (Explorer's `cytoscape.ts`, the ontology editor's `ontologyDocument.ts`). **What Canvas2D's `fillText` actually does with a right-to-left run is undocumented and had to be found empirically against a real running browser**: it already reverses a standalone right-to-left run into correct reading order on its own, but does not reposition whole runs relative to each other the way a right-to-left paragraph base direction requires — so the fix is bidi-js's fully-reordered string with each right-to-left run reversed a *second* time, undoing only the correction `fillText` was always going to make. Verified two ways: 63 unit tests (7 for `bidiLabel.ts` itself) and, because eyeballing unfamiliar Hebrew letterforms produced three different wrong reads in a row, an objective pixel cross-correlation against a real seeded server and browser — individually-rendered reference glyphs matched against column-segmented screenshot crops — confirming the rendered canvas matches native DOM `dir="auto"` exactly for a mixed right-to-left/left-to-left label (`לקוח_orders` → `orders_לקוח`). 97.62% mutation score; the 4 survivors are the RTL pre-check guards, proven equivalent for every input (the underlying bidi-js pipeline is a correct no-op on non-RTL text regardless of the guard). **Known, documented limitation**: bracket mirroring is not attempted — descoped after empirical testing showed it did not behave as hand-derived; real names carrying both a bracket and right-to-left text are rare enough that this is recorded rather than shipped unverified.
- [x] **D** `rdf:reifies` synthesised at the query surface, so the standard vocabulary returns rows rather than zero — store and flake count untouched. **Found and fixed a real pushdown bug via the end-to-end RED test**: pushdown narrowed a `rdf:reifies` pattern to the synthetic predicate itself, which no flake ever has, so the query returned zero rows before synthesis ever ran — a gap four passing dataset-level unit tests did not catch
- [x] Slices B, C and D share one `oxrdf/rdf-12` feature gate — one decision, taken once for the workspace
- [x] **`GET /graph/export/rdf` — found and fixed 8 August 2026** (`plans/EPIC-COMPLETION-PLAN.md` Phase 1.8). `graph_owl_rdf_io::StandardRdfIo::serialize` (with the Slice B `rdf:reifies` synthesis already built in) had zero callers outside its own crate's tests — no RDF export reached HTTP at all, only the five LPG-shaped formats (`GraphML`, bulk CSV, Cypher, JSON Lines, JSON Graph) did. `?format=turtle|jsonld|ntriples|nquads` (the four `RdfFormat` variants Epic 94 actually implements), scoped to the caller's authorized estate the same way its five siblings already are (`Catalog::authorized_flakes`, the flake-shaped analogue of `authorized_lpg_elements`), proven end to end in `crates/graph-owl-server/tests/graph_export.rs` against the same real bank-estate authorization fixture the GraphML test uses.
- [x] Export dialog offers RDF as one of its format choices and previews it *(UI → Epic 42)* — **shipped 9 August 2026**. `ExportDialog.tsx` lists all four RDF wire formats as their own entries alongside the five LPG shapes; full accounting under Epic 42's own Slice E section

### Epic 95 — OWL 2 RL completion
- [x] **The four RL axioms in scope beyond Epic 6's eight** *(4 August 2026)* — `owl:propertyChainAxiom`, `owl:InverseFunctionalProperty`, `owl:FunctionalProperty`, `owl:hasKey`, added to `graph-owl-reasoning`'s existing semi-naive fixpoint rather than a new engine. Property chains are decoded from an ordinary `rdf:first`/`rdf:rest`/`rdf:nil` list (arbitrary length, not hard-coded to the plan's two-property example), the same encoding OWL itself uses, reusing named nodes rather than adding blank-node support this reasoner has never needed. Every path is recomputed per pass rather than delta-tracked per chain position — a chain has few links in any ontology this project models, and the existing fixpoint's dedup already makes re-scanning safe, just not maximally cheap; what *is* delta-gated is emission, so a path built entirely of old edges is not re-emitted as new. The disjointness/cardinality/existential rules the plan scopes out stay out, unchanged. **Found a real bug while writing the fixpoint-cost test**: `hasKey`'s instance comparison walked a `HashSet` in one direction only (`i < j`), so which of `x sameAs y` / `y sameAs x` got derived depended on the process's hash seed rather than the data — passed on first run, failed intermittently on a later one. Fixed to match `InverseFunctionalProperty`'s already-correct both-directions iteration; both rules' own tests now assert both directions explicitly
- [x] Explanation panel extends to the new axioms — same surface, more rules *(UI → Epic 41, closed 5 August 2026)*. This turned out to need no production change at all: `ui/src/governance/explanation.ts`'s `flatten`/`rulesUsed` treat `rule` as an opaque string and never branch on its value, and `App.tsx`'s `DerivationChain` renders `row.rule` in a bare `<Tag>` the same way for every rule — so the four new wire values (`RuleName`'s derived `Serialize` with `rename_all = "camelCase"`: `propertyChain`, `inverseFunctionalProperty`, `functionalProperty`, `hasKey` — **not** the `rule:`-prefixed strings `as_str()` returns for metrics/CLI use) already flowed through unchanged. What was missing was proof, the same gap Epic 31's memory item closed on: no test exercised the new wire values specifically. `ui/src/governance/explanation.test.ts` now has two tests asserting all four flatten and list correctly, using the real camelCase wire names a server response actually carries

### Epic 96 — SHACL-SPARQL
- [x] **`sh:sparql`/`sh:SPARQLConstraint`, the bare constraint — shipped 8 August 2026** — against the 2017 SHACL Recommendation §5, not the 1.2 Working Draft. `Constraint::Sparql` (`graph-owl-ontology`), parsed from `sh:sparql`/`sh:select` triples (`graph-owl-constraint`); `Catalog::run_validation_as` evaluates it for real, `$this` pre-bound via a joined `VALUES` clause, through the same `execute_algebra` `Catalog::sparql` itself calls — so budget truncation and authorization apply identically to a hand-typed query. **A real authorization gap found by this slice's own two-principal test**: pre-binding `$this` via VALUES means it never appears in the query's own triple patterns, so the ordinary pushdown-based authorization scan has nothing to gate on for a query built entirely from `FILTER NOT EXISTS` — fixed with an explicit per-focus-node visibility check before a constraint is even attempted, reusing `Self::authorization_key`'s `dsc:fqn`-first resolution already established for the graph-flake path
- [x] **The bare constraint is now actually reachable — found and fixed 8 August 2026** (`plans/EPIC-COMPLETION-PLAN.md` Phase 1.1). `POST /validation/runs` called `catalog.run_validation()`, not `run_validation_as` — the only method that evaluates `sh:sparql` at all. A shape using it reported zero violations forever, through the real API, regardless of what the data said; the evaluator above was correct and unit-tested, it was simply never invoked from the live endpoint. Found by writing an HTTP-level RED test rather than trusting the existing `Catalog`-level tests, which is also how this surfaced a second, independent gap: `sh:sparql`/`sh:select` were never added to the predicate registry (`V5__shacl_predicates.sql` seeded every other SHACL predicate but not these two), so a real Postgres-backed store refused to even *assert* a `sh:sparql` shape — invisible until now because `graph_owl_api`'s own unit tests use an in-memory fake that does not enforce predicate registration. Fixed with a new migration (`V10__shacl_sparql_predicates.sql`) plus the one-line handler fix, proven end to end in `crates/graph-owl-server/tests/validation.rs`
- [ ] SPARQL-based constraint components (`sh:SPARQLConstraintComponent`, `sh:parameter`, `sh:labelTemplate`) — **explicitly not attempted this pass**, a stated scope cut against the bare constraint's own time budget, not a silent drop
- [ ] The violations workflow is unchanged; **authoring gains a second language**, so the constraint editor gains a second mode *(UI → Epic 41 Slice G)*

### Epic 97 — Incremental & parallel reasoning
- [x] Incremental maintenance rather than full recomputation. **Wired in, 8 August 2026**: `Catalog::run_reasoning_incremental(retracted, budget)` uses DRed (`derive_incremental`) against an in-memory cache of the previous run's structured result when one exists and something was actually retracted; falls back to a full run otherwise (nothing cached, or nothing retracted — both real, principled conditions rather than an invented threshold). `Catalog::run_reasoning`'s own full path, and the incremental method's fallback, now call `derive_within_parallel` instead of `derive_within` — byte-identical, so a wholesale re-derivation has no reason to run single-threaded. `ReasoningReport` gained a `technique` field (`Full`/`Incremental`) so the choice is observable, not just internal. Proven with a real premise-loss test: retracting the outer link of a two-hop chain prunes the two-hop conclusion via the incremental path while the still-supported one-hop conclusion survives. 4 new tests, 0 missed mutants on the diff. **Scope, stated rather than assumed**: this wires the invocation path only — subscribing to retraction events automatically and the `maintained_to` freshness stamp below remain separate, larger work, exactly as `97-incremental-parallel-reasoning.md`'s own "Open" section already said
- [x] **`POST /reasoning/runs` can now actually take the incremental path over HTTP — found and fixed 8 August 2026** (`plans/EPIC-COMPLETION-PLAN.md` Phase 1.9). `Catalog::run_reasoning_incremental` was correct, mutation-tested, and unreachable from a live deployment — the endpoint always did a full run, so `DRed` never executed outside `graph-owl-api`'s own unit tests. This is the **explicit, caller-supplied** path — a report of what the caller already withdrew from the base graph, not an instruction for this endpoint to withdraw it, matching `run_reasoning_incremental`'s own documented contract exactly — and it remains available for a caller with its own list (an admin tool replaying a specific retraction batch, say). **Corrected 8 August 2026**: this bullet previously claimed "the user chose explicit" for decision 4.4; that was wrong — 4.4 was decided as **server-tracked watermark, not caller-supplied** (see the next bullet), and this explicit path predates that decision rather than fulfilling it. Needed a wire encoding for `Flake`/`Sid`/`FlakeValue` that did not exist anywhere in the codebase (neither derives `Serialize`/`Deserialize`): `Sid` travels in the compact `namespace:id` form `flake_body` already renders elsewhere (not an IRI — a runtime-registered predicate has no IRI mapping at all), and the object is a tagged `{type, value}` covering the scalar shapes DRed maintenance actually needs (`ref`/`string`/`boolean`/`int`/`float`/`instant`/`uuid`); the RDF-import-specific `TripleTerm`/`LangString`/`Json`/`Bytes`/`Duration` variants are deliberately absent and rejected by name via `serde`'s own "unknown variant" error rather than silently mishandled. Proven in `crates/graph-owl-server/tests/reasoning_incremental.rs`: a retraction reported through the body takes the incremental path and prunes exactly the conclusion that lost its premise while the still-supported one survives, a malformed `Sid` names the offending field, and an unsupported value type is refused
- [x] **Decision 4.4 implemented: server-tracked retraction watermark, no caller involvement — shipped 8 August 2026.** `TripleStore::retractions_since(since)` (new trait method, default no-op — the same convention `compact`/`partition_health` already use) queries the `flakes` view directly for `op = false AND t > since`: **never a separate log**, since retractions are already durable, ordinary rows in the same append-only store `retract_flakes` writes to. `Catalog::run_reasoning_auto` is the automatic wrapper: reads the last run's own watermark from the in-memory cache, asks `retractions_since` what changed, filters out the reasoning overlay's own churn (`finish_reasoning_run` retracts the *previous* run's conclusions before writing new ones — a real retraction in the same flake log that would otherwise look exactly like a base fact disappearing), and calls `run_reasoning_incremental` with what is left. Losing the watermark (a restart — in-memory, not persisted) is always safe: the next call falls back to a full run, the identical fallback `run_reasoning_incremental` already had for "nothing cached". `POST /reasoning/runs` now calls this automatically whenever the request body carries no explicit `retracted` list — an empty body no longer always means "full run". Mutation-tested clean on all three touched crates. Verified end to end in `crates/graph-owl-server/tests/reasoning_auto.rs` against real Postgres: a retraction performed directly against the graph engine, with **no caller ever telling the endpoint about it**, is discovered and correctly pruned on the next empty-body call
- [x] **Overlay staleness is visible — shipped 8 August 2026 (Phase 3 item 3.11).** `ReasoningReport` gained `maintainedTo`, the transaction time this run accounts every retraction up to — reserved (`next_time`) *before* the run reads its base, not after, so a retraction landing mid-run stays visible to the *next* call rather than being silently treated as already accounted for. Reaches the wire on every `/reasoning/runs` response, verified directly against a real server
- [x] **Console: overlay staleness actually shown, not just on the wire — shipped 9 August 2026 (Epic 41 Slice G).** Found while re-checking Slice G's own acceptance criteria: `maintainedTo`/`technique`/`partial`/`ignoredAxioms` all reached `/reasoning/runs`'s JSON, but the Governance page's "Run reasoning" button discarded the response entirely (`.then(() => load())`, `load()` reloads only validation findings) — the wire field existed and nothing read it. Fixed: a new `ui/src/governance/reasoningRun.ts` (`describeReasoningRun`, 100% Stryker mutation score, 6 unit tests) composes the technique, counts and raw `maintainedTo` watermark into one line — no fabricated "N minutes ago": the same reason `PartitionHealthPanel` shows a raw transaction time rather than converting it, since `maintainedTo` carries no wall-clock companion either. A partial run gets its own warning-coloured alert naming how many axioms were ignored (correctly singularized for exactly one). Verified end to end in the browser: "Full run — 0 derived, 0 replaced, maintained to t=144" rendered after a real reasoning run against the demo database
