### Plan 122a — `graphowl-app` replaces `ui/` as the console (A0–A11 shipped 17–18 August 2026 — Plan 122a complete)

- [x] **A0 — shell, design tokens and a dual embed, laid down first so every later slice had somewhere real to land.** `graphowl-app/` (Vite + React + react-router-dom + Tailwind v4, `e9a2294`) with the mockup's own tokens ported verbatim into `theme.css`, a CI-asserted route budget (≤30), and `graph-owl-ui` embedding it at `/next` *beside* `ui/dist` at `/` — the dual embed let every later slice be verified live against a real server without cutting over (and thus breaking) the console still in front of users.
- [x] **A1–A10 — one slice per nav group, each verified against the real backend, not assumed from the mockup.** Global chrome (`8217302`), Overview (`983e83c`), Explore/Entity with a G6 canvas (`1898fd4`), TRACE (`7faf538`), GOVERN (`985fe0b`), INGEST (`8d4f859`), Vocabulary Studio (`cddcc33`), Agents/MCP/Runs (`d5d46ae`), Analytics gap confirmed real and left unbuilt rather than faked (`1633bd7`), PLATFORM (`7c6e76e`). **The recurring pattern, formalized after A5**: when a mockup screen showed more than the real API supports, build only the real part and ask rather than fabricate — used for Contradictions (A5) and the mapping wizard (A6). Twice the backend turned out *richer* than the plan assumed (A7's reviewer-assignment requirement on term promotion, A10's separate available-vs-installed pack identity), each found by testing live and fixed the same session rather than shipped broken.
- [x] **A11 — the cutover: `graphowl-app/dist` embedded at `/`, `ui/` archived, one console again.** Full detail in `plans/122a-graphowl-app.md`'s A11 section. Three real, previously-invisible bugs surfaced by this slice's own new tests, all fixed same session: (1) 4 of graphowl-app's 24 route names collided with real API paths (`/overview`, `/drift` silently served API JSON over `GET`; `/lineage`, `/mcp` 405'd) — invisible through A0–A10 because the console lived under `/next/`, caught by a new test that reads `routes.ts` directly rather than hardcoding names, fixed by renaming the 4 frontend slugs rather than the versioned API surface. (2) The console's first-ever live axe pass (Playwright + `@axe-core/playwright`, run against a real Postgres-backed server) found the mockup's own text-token contrast fails WCAG AA (3.96:1/3.03:1 against a 4.5:1 floor `00f` states as non-negotiable), an unlandmarked `<header>`, a missing `<h1>` (fixed with a visually-hidden one sourced from the real nav label), and 6 unlabeled `<select>` elements — all fixed, all re-verified green end to end. (3) The release binary measured 58MB against `00f`'s 50MB budget; the workspace `Cargo.toml` had no `[profile.release]` at all, so nothing stripped the symbol table — added `strip = true`, re-measured at 47MB.
- [x] **A11 — found and fixed a false positive in `scripts/check-wire-casing.py` itself while running the full gate**, unrelated to this slice's own code but blocking it: the checker flagged `RdfEditDryRun.new_inferences` and `Strategy.starts_in`, neither a real violation — one has a doc comment mentioning "ToSchema" in prose that a naive substring search misread as the type deriving it, the other is a `rename_all = "snake_case"` pack-config type with no camelCase wire to disagree with in the first place. Fixed the checker to require an actual `#[derive(... ToSchema ...)]` and to only fire on a genuinely camelCase-directed enum; verified against synthetic true-positive and true-negative cases before trusting it silent on the rest of the workspace.


### Plan 123 — Reco Now, agentic reconciliation (Slices A, B, C0 shipped 17–19 August 2026; C–I open)

- [x] **A — reconciliation correctness: multi-rate invoices, credit notes, and buckets that mean one thing each.** Every invoice on either side lands in exactly one of matched / review / only-books / only-portal, and the ITC position separates *pending* (deferred until the supplier files — recoverable) from *blocked* (s.17(5) — lost). Two states that a single "at risk" number had been averaging into one, which is the difference between chasing a supplier and writing the credit off.

- [x] **B — feed the starved rules.** Payment events, goods receipts and the portal's own ITC-availability flag reach the graph, so `gst:PaymentOverdue`, `gst:GoodsReceiptTiming` and `gst:ITCNotAvailable` have inputs to run against instead of reporting "not evaluated" forever.

- [x] **B, continued — upload-time data-quality flagging.** `app/data_quality.py`'s `inspect_rows` reads a file as uploaded and reports what is wrong with it — missing GSTINs, unparseable dates, absent invoice numbers — grouped by problem with a row count and a first example row, in plain language stating what each problem costs downstream. **Everything it reports is a warning, never a rejection**: a file with problems is still the best information available, and refusing it leaves the reviewer with nothing. Surfaced on both upload and reopen. 16 tests; one surviving mutant killed by a case that asserted the *absence* of `unparseable_date` alongside the presence of `missing_payment_date` — the original test asserted against a code that kind never emits, so it passed regardless of behaviour.

- [x] **C0 — a reconciliation reads only the graphs its own run names.** Inserted before Slice C after live comparison of two periods of one client showed a rule reporting "checked, clean" on a period whose data had never been uploaded, because a *different* period had supplied it. `in_run_scope` narrows every fact to the run's named graphs, applied **after** authorization and before the budget — two orthogonal questions kept separate ("may this principal see this fact" vs "should this evaluation be looking at it"). A fact belonging to no named graph stays in scope: schema, projection and vocabulary are not import-sourced and belong to no run, and excluding them would leave a scoped run unable to interpret the data it was given. Threaded through `scoped_facts` → `execute_algebra` → `sparql_scoped`, with `reconcile_pack`/`run_rule` passing the scope to the requirements check as well as the rule query — a requirement satisfied by another period's data is the same bug one layer down. Verified against two real periods: March unchanged (₹72,900 confirmed / ₹89,800 blocked, both rules FAILED), April correctly NOT EVALUATED for the two rules whose inputs it never uploaded. **A known, deliberately unfixed gap**: findings are stored per pack with no run tag, so two periods holding the *same* invoice numbers would still share findings; real periods do not repeat invoice numbers, and the durable fix is a run-tagged finding belonging with the findings model rather than here.

- [x] **C0, continued — the rule panel explains itself, and a finding count leads somewhere.** Two gaps a live user session named directly. (1) `RuleOutcome` gained `summary` end to end — `graph-owl-api` → HTTP → migration `0007` → `repo.py` → the React panel — because a list of labels shows a reader nothing: `gst:PaymentOverdue` means something only to whoever wrote it, "credit taken on an invoice not paid within 180 days of its date" means something to a reviewer. A rule that supplied no summary stores `NULL` and renders nothing, rather than inventing text. (2) A flagged rule's "N findings" is now a control: clicking it filters the invoice table to the invoices carrying that label, so the count is a way in rather than a dead number. `visibleRows` was extracted as a pure function to make the two independent narrowings (bucket, rule label) testable — 6 tests; Stryker is not configured for this frontend, so its mutants were reasoned through by hand rather than measured, and that is stated rather than claimed as a run.

- [x] **C0, continued — the crate's own unit tests compiled again.** `cargo test -p graph-owl-api --lib` had not compiled since the C0 commit and one before it: `scope_facts` gained a parameter and `FindingRuleDef` gained `requires`, and 18 test call sites were never updated, so 736 passing tests were invisible behind a compile error. Fixed, and the gap that let it happen closed the same session: **`in_run_scope` had no unit test at all** — C0 shipped on end-to-end verification alone, for a predicate whose permissive failure mode is a rule silently reporting on data the run was never given. 9 tests added, including the negative cases mutation testing demands (a graph the run did not name is dropped; a prefix match is *not* enough on its own; an empty scope admits only facts belonging to no graph). All 3 `in_run_scope` mutants caught, 0 missed, 0 unviable.

- [x] **C — GSTR-2A alongside 2B, and drift between them.** 2A is its own dataset kind now, ingested *alongside* 2B and never instead of it. **This reverses a documented decision** — `graphowl_client.py` said there is "no separate `Gstr2aInvoice` class, 2A is a revolving view over the same supplier-declared data `gst:Gstr1Invoice` already carries". True of the content, and it misses why 2A is held: a revolving view has a **pull date** and a filing does not. 2B freezes on the 14th and is what a claim rests on; 2A keeps moving, and the only reason to keep it is to answer "what has the portal said since". A class with no observation time cannot express that. Collapsing them also lost *who is speaking*: GSTR-1 is what the supplier says they filed, 2A is what the portal shows. `gst:Gstr2aInvoice` + `gst:Gstr2aSnapshot` (keyed by period **and** pull date, so a later pull cannot overwrite the history drift is computed from), reached by `gst:observedIn` then `gst:seenIn` — one verb per level, following `appearsIn`/`filedIn` rather than `reflectedIn`'s reuse. Two new pack rules: `gst:FiledLateInGstr2a` (in 2A, in no 2B this deployment holds) and `gst:AmendedAfterClaim` (2A value now differs from the frozen 2B, **no tolerance applied** — both numbers come from the same authority, so any difference is a real amendment rather than a rounding artefact). The two partition one join and cannot both fire on an invoice. Both declare `requires`, so absent 2A reports *not-evaluated* rather than clean. 7 Rust integration tests against the pack's real SPARQL, including the plan's own stated RED verbatim — a supplier filing in month N+1 for a month-N invoice produces the finding against month N and none once a 2B carries it. Plus GSTR-9 Table 8: 8A computed from 2A (which is how it is *defined* — a 2B-only deployment cannot compute it at all), and **every row the data cannot support reported as uncomputed with the dataset it needs named, never as zero** — a GSTR-9 is filed, and a zero nobody derived closes a question a blank would have opened.

- [x] **C — three real bugs found while building it, two of them pre-existing.** (1) **An invoice with no 2B counterpart was reported as *matched*.** `native_findings.reconcile` defaults to matched when no finding names an invoice and kept that default where there was demonstrably nothing to match against — converting "no rule fired" into "both sides agree", this product's central failure mode. On the sample fixture that was 3 unfiled invoices reading as safe and **₹44,100 of at-risk credit counted as confirmed**. Found by hand-deriving the fixture's answer key while investigating a characterisation test that had been failing for several sessions — the pin was right and the code had drifted. The old code defended itself on reverse-charge grounds, correct for *flagged* RCM invoices only; the exemption now checks the flag. (2) That same characterisation test also carried a **legitimate** change it was wrongly blocking: `matched`/`review` moved 6/1 → 7/0 because `reconciliation.py`'s fallback uses a flat ₹1 tolerance while the pack reads Rule 36(4)'s own `capPercent`, and RI-7890 differs by ₹180 on ₹13,500 (1.33%). The statutory test is the correct one. Both splits total identically and the difference is exactly RI-7890's own ₹13,500, which is what makes it explainable rather than merely current. (3) **A regression Slice C0 itself shipped**: giving `POST /packs/{pack}/reconcile` an optional scope body made a literal JSON `null` body 422 — three of this crate's own reconcile tests had been failing on it, unnoticed because the crate's tests were not being run. Fixed with `Option<Json<Option<_>>>`; `deny_unknown_fields` still refuses a mistyped key, so accepting `null` did not become accepting anything.
- [~] **D — the screens become five stages, and the GSTR-3B working paper exists.** The nav grouped by **noun** — ITC, SUPPLIERS, COMPLIANCE, DELIVER, DATA — so a reviewer had to know which category held the screen they wanted, and screens used together (Reconcile and Exceptions; ITC position and the 3B working paper) sat in different places. The five stages name **what you are doing**, in the order a period is worked: `Data → Reconcile → Cases → Intelligence → Act`, plus Home and Settings. `nav.test.ts` asserts the ordering as a property — each stage may depend only on the ones before it — because a nav in another order teaches the wrong sequence to whoever learns the product from it. **No screen was removed**: which screens should cease to exist is a product decision, and every one of the 29 is still reachable, which a test enforces. `nav.ts`'s mutation score rose 67% → 73% as a side effect.

  **The GSTR-3B working paper is built** — §4's "the deliverable". Gross ITC → s.17(5) blocked → Rules 42/43 → s.16(2)(b) timing → Rule 37 → net, with **every line naming its own source** and every statutory deduction citing the provision that required it, because a figure a reviewer cannot trace is one they have to take on trust. The filed return is shown beside the computed position and deliberately **not** netted into it: a computed claim and a filed one are different assertions, and merging them hides which is being made. A finding the chain has no line for is surfaced in `unmodelled`, never silently dropped or silently deducted.

- [x] **D — three real bugs, all found by running the endpoint against real data rather than by inspection.** (1) **The working paper overstated claimable ITC by ₹66,600** on a real period — ₹189,540 reported against a true ₹122,940. `gst:PaymentOverdue` and `gst:GoodsReceiptTiming` were both flagged and both deducted **zero**, because neither rule's evidence carried a tax amount: the queries bound the invoice but never projected `gst:taxAmount`. Both now do, and both findings declare it as evidence. A reversal nobody can size is not actionable. (2) **The route flattened an unknown amount to zero** with `or 0` before the computation ever saw it, so the endpoint reported a *complete* chain over a case whose amount nobody had established — the pure unit tests passed throughout, because they call the function directly. The same synthesis lesson this codebase has now learned three times: a unit test proves the function, only a test through the real entry point proves the caller feeds it honestly. `build_working_paper` now counts unquantified deductions separately and reports `complete: false`, and the screen says the net figure is an *upper bound*, not the answer. (3) **`gst:GoodsReceiptTiming` was landing in `unmodelled`** — s.16(2)(b) is a genuine statutory deduction and had no line in the chain.
- [ ] **E — use what graph-owl already has**: explain, resolution, validation, waivers, lineage, memories, threads, proposals.
- [ ] **F — event-triggered agents.**
- [ ] **G — alignment and analytics.**
- [ ] **H — new pack rules.**
- [ ] **I — the GraphOWL console's own search, node count and graph display.**

## Rules for this tracer

0. **This file is the single source of truth for what is built.** Per-epic
   progress lives here, in the `[x]` / `[~]` / `[ ]` marks. Each plan's
   `**Status**:` line is a *summary* of these marks and is derived from them —
   never the other way round.

   **Recorded because they had drifted badly.** On 28 July 2026, twelve plans
   said "Not started" for epics this file marked shipped — including Epics 1, 2,
   3, 15 and 39, whose work is demonstrable in Demo 1 and Demo 2, and Epic 7,
   which had three slices in `git log`. Rule 4 below is why: this file is
   updated in the same commit as the slice, and the status lines were not, so
   they drifted exactly as rule 4 predicts a separately-updated tracker will.
   The lines are corrected; the rule exists so the correction is not needed
   again. **If the two disagree, this file is right and the status line is
   stale** — the same relationship the `00*` documents have with the code.

   **Corollary, added 28 July 2026 after a second drift in the other
   direction.** A pending item must be re-checked against the code before it is
   worked on, not copied forward. Three entries here — Epic 39's facets, Epic
   39's keyboard navigation, Epic 2's `PATCH`/`DELETE` — sat as gaps for
   revisions after the code shipped, because each revision copied the previous
   revision's list. This file is authoritative over the plans' status lines; it
   is **not** authoritative over the code. Rule 4 keeps it true going forward,
   and a grep keeps it honest when it has not been.

1. **Cumulative, always.** Demo N runs everything Demo N−1 ran. A regression in an earlier demo blocks the later one.
2. **A demo is a runnable state**, not a checklist. If it cannot be shown end to end, it is not done regardless of how many boxes are ticked.
3. **`[~]` requires a named gap.** A partial tick without a stated hole is a full tick pretending to be honest.
4. **Update this file in the same commit** as the slice it records. A tracer updated separately drifts within a week.
